But anyway... yeah, I just can't seem to shake this tired feeling, no matter what I do.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down lately?\n\nPatient: Sad? Um, I don't really think so. I haven't really noticed feeling sad, exactly. \n\nIt's funny you mention being sad, though, because my neighbor just got this new dog, a basset hound, and it has the absolute saddest face I've ever seen. You know how their eyes just sort of droop down? They walk him past my ground-floor window every morning at exactly 7:15. I know the time because I always have the local morning news on in the background, and the anchor on that channel wears these incredibly bright ties. Like, neon pink or highlighter yellow. I always wonder who picks those out for him, or if he just has really loud taste. My dad used to wear awful ties to work. He had this one with little golf clubs all over it that he thought was hilarious, even though he didn't even play golf. He just liked driving the golf carts around when his boss took him out. \n\nBut anyway... no, I wouldn't really say I've been feeling down. Just that weird exhausted feeling I was talking about.\n\nDoctor: Have you been enjoying things you normally like to do?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really. I've pretty much stopped caring about the things that usually make me feel like myself. Like, I used to love sketching and going to gallery openings, but lately I just... don't care. I haven't even been cooking or doing yoga. I usually play tennis with my friend Sarah, but I've been cancelling on her. \n\nWhich makes me feel a little bad, because Sarah just got this really nice new tennis racket. She was telling me all about the grip tape she bought for it, which apparently comes in all these different neon colors now. I remember when grip tape was just black or white. My high school gym teacher, Mr. Henderson, was obsessed with keeping all the school rackets perfectly taped with this awful white tape that got dirty immediately. He was a weird guy, he used to make us run laps if we didn't tie our shoes in a double knot. He said it was a safety hazard, but I think he just liked yelling. He had this really loud whistle that he wore on a lanyard that looked like it was made out of shoelaces. \n\nBut yeah, I just don't have the energy or interest for tennis, or sketching, or anything right now.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of anything that you still enjoy doing?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. I mean, I was just saying, I stopped caring about all of it. The sketching, the galleries, cooking, yoga... even the tennis with Sarah. It's just all... whatever. \n\nIt's weird how fast things can just stop mattering to you. Like, I used to spend hours in the kitchen trying out new recipes. I had this one cookbook that was entirely based on historical recipes, like stuff they ate in medieval times. I tried making this honey-glazed turnip dish once, and it was a total disaster. The honey burnt to the bottom of the pan and it took me like three days to scrub it off. I had to use this special steel wool pad that my mom swore by. She always bought them in bulk from this warehouse store that was like an hour away from our house. We used to make a whole day trip out of it, and we'd always stop at this little diner on the way back that had the best milkshakes. They served them in those tall metal cups, so you got the extra on the side. I always got strawberry. \n\nBut no, to answer your question, I can't really think of a single thing I enjoy doing right now. It's all just too much effort.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel any pleasure when you do absolutely nothing, just sitting or lying down, without any activities?\n\nPatient: No, not even that. Lying down doesn't feel good or relaxing, it's just... existing, I guess. I mean, like I said before, I just don't care about anything that used to make me feel like me. Sketching, the galleries, cooking, yoga, tennis... none of it brings me any joy, and doing nothing doesn't bring me any joy either. \n\nIt's actually kind of frustrating to just lie there because I end up staring at my ceiling. I have those popcorn ceilings, you know? The textured ones? I don't know who invented those, but they're terrible. They collect so much dust, and if you try to clean them, the little bits just flake off and get in your eyes. My old apartment had smooth ceilings, which was so much better, except the upstairs neighbor walked like an elephant. He was this tiny guy, maybe five foot five, but he stomped around in heavy boots all day. I think he worked in construction or something. He used to leave his muddy boots right outside his door in the hallway, and the mud would dry and flake off onto the carpet. The building manager, Mrs. Higgins, used to leave passive-aggressive notes taped to his door about it. She was always leaving notes. She left one on the laundry room door once because someone used the wrong kind of detergent in the high-efficiency washer and there were suds everywhere. \n\nBut yeah, doing nothing doesn't feel pleasurable at all. It's just as empty as everything else right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your appetite lately?\n\nPatient: Um, yeah, actually. My appetite has been really off lately. Like, I'll get to the end of the day and realize all I've had is a cup of coffee and maybe a couple of bites of toast. Which reminds me, I need to buy a new toaster. The one I have now is one of those cheap plastic ones, and the lever keeps getting stuck. You have to push it down like three times before it actually catches. I bought it at this garage sale a few years ago from this lady who was moving to Florida. She had this huge collection of ceramic frogs, like, hundreds of them, just sitting on her lawn. She said she started collecting them when her husband won one for her at a carnival in 1985. I never understood why people collect things like that. It just seems like so much dusting. \n\nBut anyway, yeah, I'm just not hungry. I haven't been trying to lose weight or anything, but my jeans are definitely fitting a lot looser than they used to. I had to pull my belt to the last notch yesterday.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself moving or speaking more slowly than you usually do?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess so. On my slower days, it just feels like my body is moving through wet cement, you know? Like every step takes so much effort. Even just answering a simple question from my spouse can take me a weirdly long time. It's like my brain is wading through molasses to find the words. \n\nWhich is funny because usually I'm a pretty fast talker. My aunt used to say I talked a mile a minute when I was a kid. She was always telling me to slow down and breathe. She lived in this old Victorian house that always smelled like lavender and mothballs. She had this huge garden out back with these massive hydrangeas. They were the blue kind, which she said meant the soil was acidic. She used to put coffee grounds in the dirt to keep them that color. I tried that with a houseplant once, but I think I used too much because the whole apartment smelled like a Starbucks for a week and then the plant just died anyway. I'm really bad with plants. Succulents are supposed to be easy, but I always end up overwatering them. \n\nBut yeah, I definitely feel like I'm moving and talking in slow motion lately. It's just exhausting.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling worthless or guilty about anything lately?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really. I don't feel guilty about anything, and I don't think I'm worthless or anything like that. I mean, I know I haven't been doing much lately, but I don't feel bad about it in that way. It's just more like I don't care. \n\nMy sister, though, she feels guilty about everything. She's one of those people who apologizes when someone else bumps into her at the grocery store. We were at Trader Joe's last week—well, I dragged myself there because she insisted I needed to get out of the house—and this guy with a cart full of two-buck Chuck totally rammed into her ankle. And she apologized to him! I couldn't believe it. I would have yelled at him. \n\nSpeaking of Trader Joe's, they discontinued those dark chocolate peanut butter cups I used to love. The ones in the little plastic tub? I asked the cashier about it, and he said it was a supplier issue. It's always a supplier issue these days. Like during the pandemic when you couldn't find toilet paper anywhere. I ended up buying this weird bamboo toilet paper online, and it was so rough. It came in these individual paper wrappers with little pandas printed on them. I kept the wrappers for a while because I thought I might use them for some kind of mixed media art project, but then I remembered I haven't touched my art supplies in months, so I just threw them away. \n\nBut no, to answer your question, I don't feel guilty or worthless. Just kind of... empty.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any thoughts about death lately?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really. I haven't been thinking about dying or anything like that. I mean, I watch a lot of true crime documentaries, so death comes up a lot on TV, but not about myself. I just finished this one series about this guy who was running a fake insurance scam in the 90s, and it was wild. He had this whole elaborate setup with fake offices and everything. It reminded me of this time I tried to set up a home office in my spare bedroom. I went to IKEA and bought all this matching furniture—the white Hemnes line, you know? It took me three days to put the desk together because they gave me the wrong size Allen wrench in the little plastic bag. \n\nI ended up having to drive all the way back to the store, and you know how the IKEA parking lot is on a Saturday. It's like a war zone. I saw two people literally screaming at each other over a parking spot near the entrance. I don't know why people care so much about parking close. You're about to walk three miles through a maze of Swedish furniture anyway, what's an extra twenty feet in the parking lot? I always park in the back where it's empty. I found this really cool vintage lamp at a thrift store on the way home that day, though. It has this stained glass shade that looks like a turtle. \n\nBut anyway, no, I haven't been having any thoughts about death. Just the regular stuff.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're depressed?\n\nPatient: I don't know if I'd call it depressed, really. I mean, I don't feel like crying all the time or anything. I just feel kind of... flat. Like a soda that's been left out on the counter overnight. \n\nSpeaking of soda, I tried that new cherry cola they have at the gas station down the street. The one with the big neon sign that always flickers on the 'O'? It tastes exactly like those cherry throat lozenges my mom used to give me when I was a kid. She kept them in this little tin box in her purse, and her purse always smelled like peppermint and old receipts. She had this massive leather tote bag that weighed a ton. I don't know what she kept in there. Probably rocks. I bought a leather bag once, trying to be more professional when I was taking classes at the community college, but it got a huge water stain on it the first week because I left my water bottle open inside. It was one of those metal ones that dent if you look at them wrong. I dropped it in the library and it made the loudest noise. Everyone stared at me. \n\nBut yeah, I wouldn't say I'm depressed. Just... not really feeling much of anything right now.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: It's been about three months now, I think. This whole quiet, flat feeling where I just don't care about anything and I'm so tired all the time. It started right around the time the seasons changed. \n\nI always notice the seasons changing because the tree outside my bedroom window drops all its leaves right onto my car. It's a huge oak tree, and the leaves get stuck in the windshield wipers. I had to replace my wipers last month because they were just smearing the rain around instead of clearing it. I went to the auto parts store, and there are like fifty different sizes. Who knew? I had to look it up in this giant heavy book they had chained to the shelf. It felt like I was in a medieval library looking up a spell or something. The guy working there had this really elaborate tattoo of a dragon on his neck, and I almost asked him where he got it done, but then I remembered I don't even like needles. I tried to donate blood once in college, but I passed out before they even got the needle in. They gave me a juice box and a sticker and sent me home. I put the sticker on my laptop, but it peeled off after a week. \n\nBut yeah, it's been about three months of just feeling this heavy, tired way.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling restless or having trouble sitting still lately?\n\nPatient: No, not really restless. Actually, it's more the opposite. On my slower days, it feels like my body is moving through wet cement. Like, just getting up from the couch feels like I'm trying to walk underwater. And even answering a simple question from my spouse can take me a weirdly long time. \n\nHe asked me what I wanted for dinner the other night, and I just stared at him for what felt like five minutes because my brain just couldn't process the words. We ended up ordering pizza from that place down on 4th Street. The one with the really good garlic knots? They always give you extra marinara sauce if you ask nicely, which is great because I hate it when the knots are dry. I tried making my own garlic knots once, from this recipe I found on Pinterest. It was a total disaster. The dough didn't rise right, so they came out like little garlic-flavored hockey pucks. I think my yeast was expired. Did you know yeast can expire? I had no idea it was a living thing you keep in a jar. It kind of creeps me out now, thinking about it. Like you have millions of tiny organisms just chilling in your pantry next to the cereal. I buy the generic brand of cereal because it's exactly the same as the name brand, just in a bag instead of a box. The bags are so loud, though, when you try to open them in the morning. \n\nBut yeah, I haven't been restless. Just really, really slow and heavy.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling like you're speeding through activities or can't slow down?\n\nPatient: Well, before this heavy feeling started, I did have a few weeks where it was like I was running on a completely different engine. I had this huge burst of energy where I couldn't stop moving. I was reorganizing all the rooms in the house, making these massive lists of things to do, and I was constantly messaging people about all these art projects I wanted to start. \n\nSpeaking of art projects, I bought all this expensive watercolor paper because I saw a video of someone painting these incredible landscapes, and it looked so relaxing. But watercolor is actually really hard because you have to wait for the layers to dry, and I have zero patience for that. I ended up getting water everywhere and ruining a perfectly good sketchbook. The sketchbook had this beautiful leather cover that smelled exactly like the saddle shop my grandpa used to take me to when I was little. He loved horses. He had this old roan mare named Buttercup who was actually kind of mean, to be honest. She bit my sister once right on the shoulder. My sister still has a tiny scar from it, shaped like a horseshoe, which is kind of ironic. She tried to tell people it was a birthmark for a while in high school because she thought it sounded more mysterious. High school was so weird. I remember we had to dissect a frog in biology, and the smell of formaldehyde made me so nauseous I had to sit in the hallway for the rest of the period. The janitor was waxing the floors, and he had this big machine that looked like a giant Roomba. \n\nBut yeah, when I was in that energetic phase, I just felt so annoyed if anyone told me to slow down or take a break. I just wanted to keep going and going.\n\nDoctor: Have you been fidgeting or having trouble staying still when you're sitting or lying down?\n\nPatient: Not really fidgeting so much, no. When I had all that energy a few months ago, it was more about doing things, not just sitting there shaking my leg or whatever. Like, I started contacting all these local art galleries, drafting sections for an email newsletter I don't even have subscribers for, planning community workshops, and trying to line up freelance clients all at once. It was way more than I could actually follow through on. \n\nI actually emailed this one gallery owner downtown, the place with the really cool exposed brick walls? I love exposed brick. It makes everything look so industrial and chic. We tried to expose the brick in our living room once, but it turned out there was just drywall and some really ugly floral wallpaper from the seventies behind it. The wallpaper had these massive orange and brown daisies. Who thought that was a good idea? It took us three days to steam it all off, and my arms ached for a week. We ended up just painting the wall this shade called 'Sea Salt,' which is supposed to be calming, but honestly, it just looks like very pale toothpaste. I buy the natural toothpaste now, the kind without fluoride, because I read this article about how it's better for you. But it doesn't foam up like regular toothpaste, so it feels like you're just brushing your teeth with minty clay. \n\nBut yeah, I wasn't really fidgeting, just trying to do a million projects at the same time.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you used to enjoy, but now find you no longer take pleasure in?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely. When the heavy feeling hits, I just stop caring about the things that usually make me feel like myself. Like, I used to love sketching and going to gallery openings, but lately, I haven't picked up a pencil in weeks. I haven't even been cooking, which I normally love. I used to make this incredible lasagna from scratch. I'd even make my own ricotta cheese. Did you know how easy it is to make ricotta? You just need milk, cream, salt, and some lemon juice. You heat it up and it separates into curds and whey, kind of like that nursery rhyme with Little Miss Muffet. I always wondered what a tuffet actually was. Is it like a footstool? Or a mound of grass? Anyway, spiders are terrifying, so I don't blame her for running away. We had this huge wolf spider in our garage last week, and I swear it was the size of a saucer. My husband tried to catch it under a Tupperware container, but it was so fast. We ended up just leaving the garage door open and hoping it left on its own. I don't even like going in the garage now. \n\nBut yeah, I haven't been doing my yoga either, or even playing tennis with my friend Sarah. I just don't have the interest in any of it right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any unusual thoughts or hearing voices that others don't hear?\n\nPatient: No, nothing like that. I haven't heard any voices or had any weird thoughts. Well, I mean, I have normal thoughts, obviously, but nothing unusual. I do think a lot about how strange it is that we all just agree on what time it is. Like, daylight saving time just happened, and it completely threw off my sleep schedule. I read somewhere that cows get really stressed out by daylight saving time because they don't understand why they're being milked an hour early or late. That makes total sense to me. I feel like a cow sometimes when the clocks change. We used to go to this dairy farm when I was a kid to get fresh ice cream. They had this flavor called \"Blue Moon\" that was bright blue and tasted kind of like Froot Loops and marshmallows. I've never found it anywhere else. I tried to recreate it once using almond extract and blue food coloring, but it just tasted like sweet chemicals and dyed my tongue blue for two days. I had a dentist appointment that week, and my hygienist, Brenda, was so confused. Brenda's great, though. She always remembers that I have a sensitive tooth on the bottom left. \n\nBut no, to answer your question, no voices or anything like that. Just my own regular thoughts.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any difficulty concentrating on things like reading or watching TV?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it's been really hard to focus lately. Like, I'll sit with my planning app open on my phone, and I just stare at it. I can't decide if I should work on a class assignment, pay a bill, or start dinner, so I end up just sitting there doing none of it for a while. Speaking of dinner, I bought all these ingredients to make a stir-fry because I got this new wok. It's one of those carbon steel ones that you have to season yourself. You have to rub oil all over it and bake it in the oven until it turns black. It smoked up my whole kitchen and set off the fire alarm. Our fire alarm is so sensitive, it goes off if you even look at the toaster wrong. The dog absolutely hates it. He runs and hides under the bed and won't come out for hours, even if I try to bribe him with a piece of cheese. He loves cheddar, but only the sharp kind. If I give him mild cheddar, he just spits it out on the rug. Dogs are so funny about what they'll eat, you know? But yeah, I just can't seem to make a decision or focus enough to get anything done.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah, definitely. On my slower days, it honestly feels like my whole body is moving through wet cement, and my brain is right there with it. Like, even answering a really simple question from my husband can take me a weirdly long time. He'll ask me something super basic, like if we need more milk from the store, and I just stare at him while my brain tries to catch up and form words. 

Speaking of milk, I watched this documentary the other day about how almond milk is made, and it takes an insane amount of water to grow almonds. Like, gallons and gallons of water for just one single almond. It made me feel really guilty because I've been buying it all the time for my iced coffees. I got this cold brew maker online recently, and it's basically just a big glass pitcher with a metal mesh filter in the middle. You're supposed to use coarse ground coffee, but I accidentally used a fine espresso roast once, and it completely clogged the mesh. It took me like an hour to scrub it clean with an old toothbrush. I keep a special toothbrush under the sink just for cleaning tiny things around the house, mostly the grout in the bathroom. Grout gets so dirty so fast, especially if it's white. I really don't know why anyone chooses white grout for a bathroom floor, it's impossible to keep clean. 

But yeah, my thoughts just feel really slow and heavy right now.

She tried to tell people it was a birthmark for a while in high school because she thought it sounded more mysterious. High school was so weird. The janitor was waxing the floors, and he had this big machine that looked like a giant Roomba. \n\nBut yeah, when I was in that energetic phase, I just felt so annoyed if anyone told me to slow down or take a break. I just wanted to keep going and going.\n\nDoctor: Have you been fidgeting or having trouble staying still when you're sitting or lying down?\n\nPatient: Not really fidgeting so much, no. When I had all that energy a few months ago, it was more about doing things, not just sitting there shaking my leg or whatever. Like, I started contacting all these local art galleries, drafting sections for an email newsletter I don't even have subscribers for, planning community workshops, and trying to line up freelance clients all at once. It was way more than I could actually follow through on. \n\nI actually emailed this one gallery owner downtown, the place with the really cool exposed brick walls? I love exposed brick. It makes everything look so industrial and chic. We ended up just painting the wall this shade called 'Sea Salt,' which is supposed to be calming, but honestly, it just looks like very pale toothpaste. Well, I do get out of bed, but it's... it's just this heavy feeling. Mostly I’m here because I’m tired in a way that sleep just doesn’t fix. Like, even doing something small, like unloading the dishwasher or trying to read for my certificate class, it just feels like way too much effort. 

Speaking of the dishwasher, we had to switch the brand of detergent pods we use because the ones I usually buy were leaving this weird white film on all the glasses. It was so gross, it made the water look cloudy when you tried to drink out of them. I read online that it has something to do with hard water, and apparently our city has really hard water. I was thinking about getting a water softener installed, but the quotes we got from plumbers were just ridiculously high. One guy wanted to charge us just for coming out to look at the pipes. And our pipes are really old anyway, they're galvanized steel, I think? My husband said they'll probably all need to be replaced in the next five years, which is just... I don't even want to think about how much that's going to cost. We've been trying to save up for a new roof first, because the shingles on the back of the house are starting to curl up at the edges. 

But yeah, the tiredness is just constant, no matter how much I sleep. It's just exhausting to even think about doing things.

He was a weird guy, he used to make us run laps if we didn't tie our shoes in a double knot. He said it was a safety hazard, but I think he just liked yelling. My old apartment had smooth ceilings, which was so much better, except the upstairs neighbor walked like an elephant. He was this tiny guy, maybe five foot five, but he stomped around in heavy boots all day. I think he worked in construction or something. The building manager, Mrs. Higgins, used to leave passive-aggressive notes taped to his door about it. She was always leaving notes. She was always telling me to slow down and breathe. She lived in this old Victorian house that always smelled like lavender and mothballs. I tried that with a houseplant once, but I think I used too much because the whole apartment smelled like a Starbucks for a week and then the plant just died anyway. I'm really bad with plants. Succulents are supposed to be easy, but I always end up overwatering them. \n\nBut yeah, I definitely feel like I'm moving and talking in slow motion lately. It's just exhausting.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling worthless or guilty about anything lately?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really. I don't feel guilty about anything, and I don't think I'm worthless or anything like that. I mean, I know I haven't been doing much lately, but I don't feel bad about it in that way. It's just more like I don't care. \n\nMy sister, though, she feels guilty about everything. She's one of those people who apologizes when someone else bumps into her at the grocery store. We were at Trader Joe's last week—well, I dragged myself there because she insisted I needed to get out of the house—and this guy with a cart full of two-buck Chuck totally rammed into her ankle. And she apologized to him! I couldn't believe it. I would have yelled at him. \n\nSpeaking of Trader Joe's, they discontinued those dark chocolate peanut butter cups I used to love. The ones in the little plastic tub? I asked the cashier about it, and he said it was a supplier issue. It's always a supplier issue these days. Like during the pandemic when you couldn't find toilet paper anywhere. I ended up buying this weird bamboo toilet paper online, and it was so rough. It came in these individual paper wrappers with little pandas printed on them. I kept the wrappers for a while because I thought I might use them for some kind of mixed media art project, but then I remembered I haven't touched my art supplies in months, so I just threw them away. \n\nBut no, to answer your question, I don't feel guilty or worthless. Just kind of... empty.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any thoughts about death lately?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really. I haven't been thinking about dying or anything like that. I mean, I watch a lot of true crime documentaries, so death comes up a lot on TV, but not about myself. I just finished this one series about this guy who was running a fake insurance scam in the 90s, and it was wild. He had this whole elaborate setup with fake offices and everything. It reminded me of this time I tried to set up a home office in my spare bedroom. I went to IKEA and bought all this matching furniture—the white Hemnes line, you know? It took me three days to put the desk together because they gave me the wrong size Allen wrench in the little plastic bag. \n\nI ended up having to drive all the way back to the store, and you know how the IKEA parking lot is on a Saturday. It's like a war zone. I saw two people literally screaming at each other over a parking spot near the entrance. I don't know why people care so much about parking close. You're about to walk three miles through a maze of Swedish furniture anyway, what's an extra twenty feet in the parking lot? I always park in the back where it's empty. I found this really cool vintage lamp at a thrift store on the way home that day, though. It has this stained glass shade that looks like a turtle. \n\nBut anyway, no, I haven't been having any thoughts about death. Just the regular stuff.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're depressed?\n\nPatient: I don't know if I'd call it depressed, really. I mean, I don't feel like crying all the time or anything. I just feel kind of... flat. Like a soda that's been left out on the counter overnight. \n\nSpeaking of soda, I tried that new cherry cola they have at the gas station down the street. The one with the big neon sign that always flickers on the 'O'? It tastes exactly like those cherry throat lozenges my mom used to give me when I was a kid. She kept them in this little tin box in her purse, and her purse always smelled like peppermint and old receipts. She had this massive leather tote bag that weighed a ton. I don't know what she kept in there. Probably rocks. I bought a leather bag once, trying to be more professional when I was taking classes at the community college, but it got a huge water stain on it the first week because I left my water bottle open inside. It was one of those metal ones that dent if you look at them wrong. I dropped it in the library and it made the loudest noise. Everyone stared at me. \n\nBut yeah, I wouldn't say I'm depressed. Just... not really feeling much of anything right now.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: It's been about three months now, I think. This whole quiet, flat feeling where I just don't care about anything and I'm so tired all the time. It started right around the time the seasons changed. \n\nI always notice the seasons changing because the tree outside my bedroom window drops all its leaves right onto my car. It's a huge oak tree, and the leaves get stuck in the windshield wipers. I had to replace my wipers last month because they were just smearing the rain around instead of clearing it. I went to the auto parts store, and there are like fifty different sizes. Who knew? I had to look it up in this giant heavy book they had chained to the shelf. It felt like I was in a medieval library looking up a spell or something. The guy working there had this really elaborate tattoo of a dragon on his neck, and I almost asked him where he got it done, but then I remembered I don't even like needles. I tried to donate blood once in college, but I passed out before they even got the needle in. They gave me a juice box and a sticker and sent me home. I put the sticker on my laptop, but it peeled off after a week. \n\nBut yeah, it's been about three months of just feeling this heavy, tired way.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling restless or having trouble sitting still lately?\n\nPatient: No, not really restless. Actually, it's more the opposite. On my slower days, it feels like my body is moving through wet cement. Like, just getting up from the couch feels like I'm trying to walk underwater. And even answering a simple question from my spouse can take me a weirdly long time. \n\nHe asked me what I wanted for dinner the other night, and I just stared at him for what felt like five minutes because my brain just couldn't process the words. We ended up ordering pizza from that place down on 4th Street. The one with the really good garlic knots? They always give you extra marinara sauce if you ask nicely, which is great because I hate it when the knots are dry. I tried making my own garlic knots once, from this recipe I found on Pinterest. It was a total disaster. The dough didn't rise right, so they came out like little garlic-flavored hockey pucks. I think my yeast was expired. Did you know yeast can expire? I had no idea it was a living thing you keep in a jar. It kind of creeps me out now, thinking about it. Like you have millions of tiny organisms just chilling in your pantry next to the cereal. I buy the generic brand of cereal because it's exactly the same as the name brand, just in a bag instead of a box. The bags are so loud, though, when you try to open them in the morning. \n\nBut yeah, I haven't been restless. Just really, really slow and heavy.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling like you're speeding through activities or can't slow down?\n\nPatient: Well, before this heavy feeling started, I did have a few weeks where it was like I was running on a completely different engine. I had this huge burst of energy where I couldn't stop moving. I was reorganizing all the rooms in the house, making these massive lists of things to do, and I was constantly messaging people about all these art projects I wanted to start. \n\nSpeaking of art projects, I bought all this expensive watercolor paper because I saw a video of someone painting these incredible landscapes, and it looked so relaxing. But watercolor is actually really hard because you have to wait for the layers to dry, and I have zero patience for that. I ended up getting water everywhere and ruining a perfectly good sketchbook. The sketchbook had this beautiful leather cover that smelled exactly like the saddle shop my grandpa used to take me to when I was little. He loved horses. He had this old roan mare named Buttercup who was actually kind of mean, to be honest. She bit my sister once right on the shoulder. My sister still has a tiny scar from it, shaped like a horseshoe, which is kind of ironic. She tried to tell people it was a birthmark for a while in high school because she thought it sounded more mysterious. High school was so weird. I remember we had to dissect a frog in biology, and the smell of formaldehyde made me so nauseous I had to sit in the hallway for the rest of the period. The janitor was waxing the floors, and he had this big machine that looked like a giant Roomba. \n\nBut yeah, when I was in that energetic phase, I just felt so annoyed if anyone told me to slow down or take a break. I just wanted to keep going and going.\n\nDoctor: Have you been fidgeting or having trouble staying still when you're sitting or lying down?\n\nPatient: Not really fidgeting so much, no. When I had all that energy a few months ago, it was more about doing things, not just sitting there shaking my leg or whatever. Like, I started contacting all these local art galleries, drafting sections for an email newsletter I don't even have subscribers for, planning community workshops, and trying to line up freelance clients all at once. It was way more than I could actually follow through on. \n\nI actually emailed this one gallery owner downtown, the place with the really cool exposed brick walls? I love exposed brick. It makes everything look so industrial and chic. We tried to expose the brick in our living room once, but it turned out there was just drywall and some really ugly floral wallpaper from the seventies behind it. The wallpaper had these massive orange and brown daisies. Who thought that was a good idea? It took us three days to steam it all off, and my arms ached for a week. We ended up just painting the wall this shade called 'Sea Salt,' which is supposed to be calming, but honestly, it just looks like very pale toothpaste. I buy the natural toothpaste now, the kind without fluoride, because I read this article about how it's better for you. But it doesn't foam up like regular toothpaste, so it feels like you're just brushing your teeth with minty clay. \n\nBut yeah, I wasn't really fidgeting, just trying to do a million projects at the same time.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you used to enjoy, but now find you no longer take pleasure in?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely. When the heavy feeling hits, I just stop caring about the things that usually make me feel like myself. Like, I used to love sketching and going to gallery openings, but lately, I haven't picked up a pencil in weeks. I haven't even been cooking, which I normally love. I used to make this incredible lasagna from scratch. I'd even make my own ricotta cheese. Did you know how easy it is to make ricotta? You just need milk, cream, salt, and some lemon juice. You heat it up and it separates into curds and whey, kind of like that nursery rhyme with Little Miss Muffet. I always wondered what a tuffet actually was. Is it like a footstool? Or a mound of grass? Anyway, spiders are terrifying, so I don't blame her for running away. We had this huge wolf spider in our garage last week, and I swear it was the size of a saucer. My husband tried to catch it under a Tupperware container, but it was so fast. We ended up just leaving the garage door open and hoping it left on its own. I don't even like going in the garage now. \n\nBut yeah, I haven't been doing my yoga either, or even playing tennis with my friend Sarah. I just don't have the interest in any of it right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any unusual thoughts or hearing voices that others don't hear?\n\nPatient: No, nothing like that. I haven't heard any voices or had any weird thoughts. Well, I mean, I have normal thoughts, obviously, but nothing unusual. I do think a lot about how strange it is that we all just agree on what time it is. Like, daylight saving time just happened, and it completely threw off my sleep schedule. I read somewhere that cows get really stressed out by daylight saving time because they don't understand why they're being milked an hour early or late. That makes total sense to me. I feel like a cow sometimes when the clocks change. We used to go to this dairy farm when I was a kid to get fresh ice cream. They had this flavor called \"Blue Moon\" that was bright blue and tasted kind of like Froot Loops and marshmallows. I've never found it anywhere else. I tried to recreate it once using almond extract and blue food coloring, but it just tasted like sweet chemicals and dyed my tongue blue for two days. I had a dentist appointment that week, and my hygienist, Brenda, was so confused. Brenda's great, though. She always remembers that I have a sensitive tooth on the bottom left. \n\nBut no, to answer your question, no voices or anything like that. Just my own regular thoughts.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any difficulty concentrating on things like reading or watching TV?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it's been really hard to focus lately. Like, I'll sit with my planning app open on my phone, and I just stare at it. I can't decide if I should work on a class assignment, pay a bill, or start dinner, so I end up just sitting there doing none of it for a while. Speaking of dinner, I bought all these ingredients to make a stir-fry because I got this new wok. It's one of those carbon steel ones that you have to season yourself. You have to rub oil all over it and bake it in the oven until it turns black. It smoked up my whole kitchen and set off the fire alarm. Our fire alarm is so sensitive, it goes off if you even look at the toaster wrong. The dog absolutely hates it. He runs and hides under the bed and won't come out for hours, even if I try to bribe him with a piece of cheese. He loves cheddar, but only the sharp kind. If I give him mild cheddar, he just spits it out on the rug. Dogs are so funny about what they'll eat, you know? But yeah, I just can't seem to make a decision or focus enough to get anything done.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your thoughts are slower than they used to be?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely. On my slower days, it honestly feels like my whole body is moving through wet cement, and my brain is right there with it. Like, even answering a really simple question from my husband can take me a weirdly long time. He'll ask me something super basic, like if we need more milk from the store, and I just stare at him while my brain tries to catch up and form words. \n\nSpeaking of milk, I watched this documentary the other day about how almond milk is made, and it takes an insane amount of water to grow almonds. Like, gallons and gallons of water for just one single almond. It made me feel really guilty because I've been buying it all the time for my iced coffees. I got this cold brew maker online recently, and it's basically just a big glass pitcher with a metal mesh filter in the middle. You're supposed to use coarse ground coffee, but I accidentally used a fine espresso roast once, and it completely clogged the mesh. It took me like an hour to scrub it clean with an old toothbrush. I keep a special toothbrush under the sink just for cleaning tiny things around the house, mostly the grout in the bathroom. Grout gets so dirty so fast, especially if it's white. I really don't know why anyone chooses white grout for a bathroom floor, it's impossible to keep clean. \n\nBut yeah, my thoughts just feel really slow and heavy right now.\n\nDoctor: Would you say your current feelings of tiredness and lack of interest in activities are interfering with your daily life and relationships?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it definitely is. I mean, I’ve completely fallen behind in my certificate coursework. I just can't seem to get anything done. I've let all the bills pile up, and cooking meals or doing basic household tasks just... isn't happening. I can't keep my plans organized or follow through on anything. My husband has had to step in and do way more around the house lately. \n\nHe actually just had to fix the vacuum cleaner because it stopped picking anything up. It turned out there was a massive hairball wrapped around the roller brush. I don't even know how it got that bad, considering I haven't been vacuuming. He had to use scissors to cut it all out. It’s a Dyson, one of those cordless ones that you mount on the wall. We got it on sale during Black Friday a couple of years ago. I love that it’s cordless, but the battery life is terrible. You get maybe twenty minutes before it dies, and then it takes hours to recharge. It’s really annoying if you're trying to do the whole house at once. We have hardwood floors mostly, but there's a big area rug in the living room that sheds like crazy. It's wool, and I read that wool rugs can shed for months after you buy them. I almost want to just roll it up and put it in the basement, but our basement gets really damp in the spring. We had to buy a dehumidifier down there because some old cardboard boxes I had stored got completely ruined. They were full of old college textbooks that I probably should have just sold back anyway. \n\nBut yeah, I’ve just pulled way back from everything. I haven't been seeing my friends, and I’ve completely dropped my art stuff, which I normally really care about. It's just too much right now.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're at a point where you can't even get out of bed in the morning?\n\nPatient: Well, I do get out of bed, but it's... it's just this heavy feeling. Mostly I’m here because I’m tired in a way that sleep just doesn’t fix. Like, even doing something small, like unloading the dishwasher or trying to read for my certificate class, it just feels like way too much effort. \n\nSpeaking of the dishwasher, we had to switch the brand of detergent pods we use because the ones I usually buy were leaving this weird white film on all the glasses. It was so gross, it made the water look cloudy when you tried to drink out of them. I read online that it has something to do with hard water, and apparently our city has really hard water. I was thinking about getting a water softener installed, but the quotes we got from plumbers were just ridiculously high. One guy wanted to charge us just for coming out to look at the pipes. And our pipes are really old anyway, they're galvanized steel, I think? My husband said they'll probably all need to be replaced in the next five years, which is just... I don't even want to think about how much that's going to cost. We've been trying to save up for a new roof first, because the shingles on the back of the house are starting to curl up at the edges. \n\nBut yeah, the tiredness is just constant, no matter how much I sleep. It's just exhausting to even think about doing things."
